Done well, it reads as effortless; done poorly, it reads as costume. The difference lies in restraint — three pieces, chosen with intention, will always outperform five chosen without.","Begin with a foundation of fine merino or a crisp cotton poplin. These base layers should sit close to the body and disappear underneath everything else. Their job is not to be seen, but to be felt — a collar peeking precisely, a cuff breaking exactly at the wrist bone.","The mid-layer carries the silhouette. An unstructured blazer, a chore jacket, or a fine-gauge cardigan introduces texture without bulk. Keep tonal: sand over ecru, charcoal over slate. Let one piece break the palette — a scarf, a belt, a sock.","Finally, the outer layer sets the tone. A tailored wool coat sharpens; a washed denim jacket softens. Proportion is everything here — if the base is slim, let the coat oversize. If the base is relaxed, keep the outer layer precise.","The result should look inevitable, as if you simply reached for what was closest. The result is a tighter, more characterful twill that fades with your life, not with a factory wash.","Fit comes down to two decisions: the rise and the leg. A mid-rise with a straight leg flatters nearly everyone; a high-rise elongates; a relaxed leg needs a considered hem.
